## GHSA-628f-v4f6-p37r · contao/comments-bundle

2026-10-09 · PHP · **CRITICAL** 9.3 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because user-supplied data, specifically the 'website' and 'email' fields in comments, was not properly sanitized before being embedded into HTML attributes like 'href' and 'title'. While 'title' attributes were already sanitized, the 'href' attribute for the email link and the 'href' attribute for the website link were not, allowing for injection of malicious scripts.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary client-side scripts into the web page, leading to session hijacking, defacement, redirection to malicious sites, or other client-side attacks against users viewing the comments.

### Key diff

```
--- a/comments-bundle/contao/dca/tl_comments.php
+++ b/comments-bundle/contao/dca/tl_comments.php
- <div class="cte_type ' . $key . '"><a href="mailto:' . Idna::decodeEmail($arrRow['email']) . '" title="' . StringUtil::specialchars(Idna::decodeEmail($arrRow['email'])) . '">' . $arrRow['name'] . '</a>' . ($arrRow['website'] ? ' (<a href="' . $arrRow['website'] . '" title="' . StringUtil::specialchars($arrRow['website']) . '" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">' . $GLOBALS['TL_LANG']['MSC']['com_website'] . '</a>)' : '') . ' – ' . Date::parse(Config::get('datimFormat'), $arrRow['date']) . ' – IP ' . StringUtil::specialchars($arrRow['ip']) . '<br>' . $title . '</div>
+ <div class="cte_type ' . $key . '"><a href="mailto:' . StringUtil::specialchars(Idna::decodeEmail($arrRow['email'])) . '" title="' . StringUtil::specialchars(Idna::decodeEmail($arrRow['email'])) . '">' . $arrRow['name'] . '</a>' . ($arrRow['website'] ? ' (<a href="' . StringUtil::specialcharsUrl($arrRow['website']) . '" title="' . StringUtil::specialchars($arrRow['website']) . '" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">' . $GLOBALS['TL_LANG']['MSC']['com_website'] . '</a>)' : '') . ' – ' . Date::parse(Config::get('datimFormat'), $arrRow['date']) . ' – IP ' . StringUtil::specialchars($arrRow['ip']) . '<br>' . $title . '</div>
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by applying proper HTML entity encoding to the 'href' attribute of the email link using `StringUtil::specialchars()` and to the 'href' attribute of the website link using `StringUtil::specialcharsUrl()`. This ensures that any special characters in user-supplied URLs are escaped, preventing them from being interpreted as active content.

## GHSA-x34j-47hf-4xg7 · @tinacms/app

2026-10-09 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.3 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The TinaCMS admin panel's preview iframe loaded its content based on a URL fragment (splat parameter) without proper validation of the origin. An attacker could craft a URL that would cause the iframe to load content from an arbitrary, attacker-controlled origin, effectively bypassing the same-origin policy.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious content into the preview iframe, leading to phishing attacks, credential theft, or other client-side attacks against users of the TinaCMS admin panel. This is a critical vulnerability due to the potential for complete compromise of the user's session.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/@tinacms/app/src/lib/preview-origin.ts
+++ b/packages/@tinacms/app/src/lib/preview-origin.ts
-export const getExpectedPreviewOrigin = (
-  url: string,
-  baseOrigin: string = typeof window !== 'undefined'
-    ? window.location.origin
-    : ''
-): string => {
-  try {
-    return new URL(url, baseOrigin || undefined).origin;
-  } catch {
-    return baseOrigin;
-  }
-};
+export const getPreviewOrigin = (): string =>
+  typeof window !== 'undefined' ? window.location.origin : '';
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `resolvePreviewPath` function that strictly validates the origin of the preview URL. It ensures that the resolved URL remains on the same origin as the admin panel. The `getExpectedPreviewOrigin` function was removed and replaced with `getPreviewOrigin`, which always returns the admin's own origin, preventing the preview origin from being derived from user-controlled input.

## GHSA-33rr-wq23-g6gg · github.com/0xJacky/Nginx-UI

2026-10-09 · Go · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `CSRF→STATE_CHANGE`

### Root cause

The Nginx-UI application allowed authentication tokens to be retrieved from cookies if the 'AuthRequired' header was missing. This fallback mechanism meant that if an API endpoint did not explicitly check for the 'AuthRequired' header, it could be vulnerable to CSRF attacks, as the browser would automatically send the session cookie with cross-site requests.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious website that, when visited by an authenticated Nginx-UI user, would trigger requests to the Nginx-UI management APIs. This could lead to unauthorized actions being performed on behalf of the user, such as modifying Nginx configurations or managing nodes.

### Key diff

```
Before: func AuthRequired(c *gin.Context) {
	token := c.GetHeader("AuthRequired")
	if token == "" {
		cookie, err := c.Cookie("AuthRequired")
		if err == nil {
			token = cookie
		}
	}
After: func AuthRequired(c *gin.Context) {
	token := c.GetHeader("AuthRequired")
	if token == "" {
		c.AbortWithStatus(http.StatusUnauthorized)
		return
	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the fallback mechanism that allowed authentication tokens to be read from cookies when the 'AuthRequired' header was absent. It enforces that authentication tokens must be present in the 'AuthRequired' header for API requests, preventing browsers from automatically attaching session cookies to cross-site requests and thus mitigating CSRF.

## GHSA-h246-wpgf-vmq5 · github.com/0xJacky/Nginx-UI

2026-10-09 · Go · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The previous fix for CVE-2026-84315 was incomplete. Specifically, the `/api/cluster` router, which handles sensitive node pairing and credential rotation operations, was not protected by the `RequireSecureSession` middleware. This allowed these critical operations to be performed without the necessary One-Time Password (OTP) step-up authentication.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the intended OTP step-up authentication for sensitive cluster operations. This could lead to unauthorized node pairing, credential rotation, or other administrative actions within the Nginx-UI cluster, potentially compromising the entire system.

### Key diff

```
--- a/api/cluster/node_auth.go
+++ b/api/cluster/node_auth.go
@@ -0,0 +1,562 @@
+package cluster
+
+import (
+...
+func CreatePairingCode(c *gin.Context) {
+...
+func CompletePairing(c *gin.Context) {
+...
+func PairNode(c *gin.Context) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new file `api/cluster/node_auth.go` which contains the handlers for node authentication and pairing. The key part of the fix, though not directly visible in the provided diff, is the wrapping of the `/api/cluster` router endpoints with `RequireSecureSession` to enforce OTP step-up authentication for sensitive operations like `CreatePairingCode`, `CompletePairing`, `PairNode`, and `RotateCredential`.

## GHSA-j6cw-g6p4-7hch · github.com/argoproj/argo-cd/v3

2026-10-09 · Go · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `ProxyCommand` option in SSH configuration, when used with a SOCKS5 proxy URL, directly embedded the hostname and port from the URL into a shell-interpreted string. An attacker could craft a malicious SOCKS5 proxy URL containing shell metacharacters, which would then be executed as part of the `connect-proxy` command, leading to arbitrary command execution.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary command execution on the Argo CD repo-server by providing a specially crafted SOCKS5 proxy URL for a Git repository. This could lead to full compromise of the repo-server, allowing access to sensitive data or further attacks within the cluster.

### Key diff

```
--- a/util/git/creds.go
+++ b/util/git/creds.go
@@ -373,17 +438,56 @@ func (c SSHCreds) Environ() (io.Closer, []string, error) {
 	// Handle SSH socks5 proxy settings
-	proxyEnv := []string{}
+	var proxyEnv []string
+	var proxyScriptPath string
 	if c.proxy != "" {
+		if err := validateProxyURL(c.proxy); err != nil {
+			sshCloser.Close()
+			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("unsafe proxy URL rejected: %w", err)
+		}
 		parsedProxyURL, err := url.Parse(c.proxy)
 		if err != nil {
 			sshCloser.Close()
 			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to set environment variables related to socks5 proxy, could not parse proxy URL '%s': %w", c.proxy, err)
 		}
-		args = append(args, "-o", fmt.Sprintf("ProxyCommand='connect-proxy -S %s:%s -5 %%h %%p'",
+
+		// Write a temporary wrapper script instead of embedding the hostname and port directly into the
+		// shell-interpreted GIT_SSH_COMMAND string.
+		proxyScriptContent := fmt.Sprintf(
+			"#!/bin/sh\nexec connect-proxy -5 -S %s:%s \"$@\"\n",
 			parsedProxyURL.Hostname(),
 			parsedProxyURL.Port()))
+		)
+
+		proxyScript, err := os.CreateTemp(argoio.TempDir, "argocd-proxy-cmd-*")
+		if err != nil {
+			sshCloser.Close()
+			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create proxy command script: %w", err)
+		}
+		proxyScriptPath = proxyScript.Name()
+
+		if _, err := proxyScript.WriteString(proxyScriptContent); err != nil {
+			_ = os.Remove(proxyScriptPath)
+			sshCloser.Close()
+			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to write proxy command script: %w", err)
+		}
+		if err := proxyScript.Close(); err != nil {
+			_ = os.Remove(proxyScriptPath)
+			sshCloser.Close()
+			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to close proxy command script: %w", err)
+		}
+		if err := os.Chmod(proxyScriptPath, 0o700); err != nil {
+			_ = os.Remove(proxyScriptPath)
+			sshCloser.Close()
+			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to chmod proxy command script: %w", err)
+		}
+
+		args = append(args, "-o", "ProxyCommand="+proxyScriptPath+" %h %p")
 		if parsedProxyURL.User != nil {
 			proxyEnv = append(proxyEnv, "SOCKS5_USER="+parsedProxyURL.User.Username())
 			if socks5Passwd, isPasswdSet := parsedProxyURL.User.Password(); isPasswdSet {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces strict validation for proxy URLs, ensuring that hostnames only contain safe characters and ports are valid. Crucially, instead of directly embedding the hostname and port into the `ProxyCommand` string, it now writes a temporary wrapper script containing these values and then uses the script's path in `ProxyCommand`, preventing shell metacharacter injection.

## GHSA-pvgv-gcp7-v38g · github.com/0xJacky/Nginx-UI

2026-10-09 · Go · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→LOG_EXPOSURE`

### Root cause

The Nginx UI application was designed to allow node pairing via a URL query parameter containing a sensitive pairing code. This design choice inherently risked exposing the credential in server logs, browser history, or referrer headers, making it vulnerable to unintended disclosure.

### Impact

An attacker could obtain the sensitive pairing code from various sources (e.g., server logs, browser history, network sniffers) and use it to complete the pairing process, potentially gaining unauthorized access to the Nginx UI node.

### Key diff

```
The provided diff shows the creation of a new file `api/cluster/node_auth.go` which implements a new secure pairing mechanism. The key change is the introduction of `CreatePairingCode` and `CompletePairing` functions that handle the pairing code in the request body, rather than URL parameters.

```go
// Old (implied, not in diff but described by advisory):
// GET /api/node/pair?code=SENSITIVE_CODE

// New (from diff):
// POST /api/node/pair/create
// POST /api/node/pair/complete (with code in request body)
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the functionality of passing the pairing code via a URL query parameter. Instead, it introduces a new API endpoint for creating pairing codes and another for completing the pairing, where the code is sent securely in the request body.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 10 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, Go, Python, C#, JavaScript

## GHSA-45gv-9wjv-xh7p · github.com/0xJacky/Nginx-UI

2026-10-09 · Go · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The application's login process did not correctly enforce passkey-based two-factor authentication (2FA) when it was enabled for a user. Specifically, if a user had a passkey configured, the system would still allow a successful password-only login without requiring the passkey verification, effectively bypassing the second factor.

### Impact

An attacker who obtains a user's password could gain full access to their account, even if the user had enabled passkey 2FA, by simply logging in with the password without needing to provide the passkey.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/user/user.go
+++ b/internal/user/user.go
@@ -177,7 +178,36 @@ type AccessTokenPayload struct {
 	ShortToken string `json="short_token,omitempty"`
 }
 
+type LoginProof string
+
+const (
+	LoginProofPassword LoginProof = "password"
+	LoginProofOTP      LoginProof = "otp"
+	LoginProofPasskey  LoginProof = "passkey"
+	LoginProofExternal LoginProof = "external"
+	LoginProofSystem   LoginProof = "system"
+)
+
+var ErrPasskeyRequired = errors.New("passkey verification is required")
+
+// IssueLoginToken is the policy gate for every interactive login path.
+func IssueLoginToken(user *model.User, proof LoginProof) (*AccessTokenPayload, error) {
+	if user == nil {
+		return nil, errors.New("user is required")
+	}
+	if proof == LoginProofPassword && !user.EnabledOTP() && user.EnabledPasskey() {
+		return nil, ErrPasskeyRequired
+	}
+	return generateJWT(user)
+}
+
+// GenerateJWT remains available for non-login system flows and tests. New
+// interactive login paths must call IssueLoginToken with an explicit proof.
 func GenerateJWT(user *model.User) (*AccessTokenPayload, error) {
+	return IssueLoginToken(user, LoginProofSystem)
+}
+
+func generateJWT(user *model.User) (*AccessTokenPayload, error) {
 	now := time.Now()
 	claims := JWTClaims{
 		Name:   user.Name,
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `IssueLoginToken` function that acts as a policy gate for all interactive login paths. This function checks if a user has a passkey enabled and, if so, explicitly requires passkey verification after a password login attempt, preventing the issuance of a JWT token without it. It also modifies the `Login` API endpoint to initiate a passkey pre-authentication flow if a passkey is enabled for the user.

## GHSA-r44w-v6gf-x3p6 · pyload-ng

2026-10-09 · Python · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The API key cache ( _apikey_cache) was incorrectly indexed by 'key_id' instead of the full 'apikey' string. This meant that if an attacker knew a valid 'key_id' (which is an integer and easier to guess or enumerate than a full API key), they could potentially bypass authentication if a valid API key with that 'key_id' had been previously cached, even without knowing the actual API key.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass API key authentication, potentially gaining unauthorized access to API functionalities and sensitive data or actions, depending on the privileges associated with the compromised API key.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/pyload/core/api/__init__.py
+++ b/src/pyload/core/api/__init__.py
-        self._apikey_cache = {}  # Format: {key_id: (timestamp, data)}
+        self._apikey_cache = {}  # Format: {apikey: (timestamp, data)}
...
-                if key_id in self._apikey_cache:
-                    cache_time, cached_data = self._apikey_cache[key_id]
+                if apikey in self._apikey_cache:
+                    cache_time, cached_data = self._apikey_cache[apikey]
```

### Fix summary

The patch corrects the API key cache implementation by changing the cache key from 'key_id' to the full 'apikey' string. This ensures that cache lookups and invalidations are performed using the complete, secret API key, preventing authentication bypasses based on knowing only the 'key_id'.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 82 occurrences**

## GHSA-662p-52hx-cmh2 · github.com/0xJacky/Nginx-UI

2026-10-09 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The application's self-upgrade mechanism downloaded and executed new binaries based solely on a digest check from the same origin as the binary itself. This meant that if the download server was compromised or a Man-in-the-Middle (MITM) attack occurred, an attacker could replace the legitimate binary with a malicious one and provide a matching digest, leading to arbitrary code execution on the server running Nginx-UI.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the server where Nginx-UI is installed, potentially leading to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or further network penetration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/upgrader/upgrade.go
+++ b/internal/upgrader/upgrade.go
@@ -146,6 +166,13 @@ func (u *Upgrader) DownloadLatestRelease(progressChan chan float64) (tarName str
 		return
 	}
 
+	// authenticity signature
+	signatureAsset, ok := assetsMap[assetName+".minisig"]
+	if !ok || signatureAsset.BrowserDownloadUrl == "" {
+		err = ErrSignatureEmpty
+		return
+	}
+
 	// digest
 	digest, ok := assetsMap[fmt.Sprintf("nginx-ui-%s.tar.gz.digest", arch.Name)]
 	if !ok || digest.BrowserDownloadUrl == "" {
@@ -176,11 +203,32 @@ func (u *Upgrader) DownloadLatestRelease(progressChan chan float64) (tarName str
 		err = errors.Wrap(err, "service.DownloadLatestRelease downloadFile error")
 		return
 	}
+	defer func() {
+		if err != nil {
+			_ = os.Remove(tarName)
+			_ = os.Remove(tarName + ".minisig")
+		}
+	}()
+
+	signatureBytes, err := downloadMetadata(signatureAsset.BrowserDownloadUrl, 64<<10)
+	if err != nil {
+		err = errors.Wrap(err, "upgrader core download signature fail")
+		return
+	}
+	keyID, err := verifyArchiveSignature(tarName, signatureBytes)
+	if err != nil {
+		return
+	}
+	if err = os.WriteFile(tarName+".minisig", signatureBytes, 0o600); err != nil {
+		err = errors.Wrap(err, "stage verified upgrader signature")
+		return
+	}
+	logger.Debug("DownloadLatestRelease verified Minisign key", fmt.Sprintf("%016X", keyID))
 
 	// check tar digest
-	digestFileBytes, err := io.ReadAll(resp.Body)
+	digestFileBytes, err := downloadMetadata(digest.BrowserDownloadUrl, 4<<10)
 	if err != nil {
-		err = errors.Wrap(err, "digest file content read error")
+		err = errors.Wrap(err, "upgrader core download digest fail")
 		return
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces Minisign signature verification for downloaded release archives. It now downloads a separate signature file and verifies the authenticity of the downloaded tarball using a trusted public key before proceeding with the upgrade. Additionally, it refactors metadata downloads into a new `downloadMetadata` function with size limits and improves error handling.

## GHSA-6r69-c6wg-7g8m · github.com/jhaals/yopass

2026-10-09 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application used Prometheus metrics middleware which included HTTP method labels without proper sanitization or normalization. An attacker could send requests with arbitrary, non-standard HTTP methods, causing the Prometheus metrics system to create an unbounded number of unique time series, each consuming memory.

### Impact

An attacker could exhaust the server's memory by sending requests with a large number of unique HTTP method strings, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/server/server.go
+++ b/pkg/server/server.go
@@ -873,7 +873,8 @@ func newMetricsMiddleware(reg prometheus.Registerer) func(http.Handler) http.Han
 func normalizedMethod(method string) string {
 	switch method {
 	case http.MethodGet, http.MethodHead, http.MethodPost, http.MethodPut,
-		http.MethodPatch, http.MethodDelete, http.MethodOptions:
+		http.MethodPatch, http.MethodDelete, http.MethodConnect,
+		http.MethodOptions, http.MethodTrace:
 		return method
 	}
 	return "<other>"
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `normalizedMethod` function to explicitly whitelist a comprehensive set of standard HTTP methods. Any HTTP method not in this predefined list will now be categorized as '<other>', preventing the creation of an unbounded number of unique metric labels.

## GHSA-889w-m37p-88m5 · pyload-ng

2026-10-09 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The application's API endpoints for user management (removing users, changing passwords, setting permissions) did not explicitly invalidate the sessions of the affected users. This meant that even after a user's permissions were changed or their account was removed, their existing session remained active, allowing them to retain old privileges or continue accessing the system.

### Impact

An attacker could exploit this by, for example, having an administrator demote their account, but then continue to use their existing session to maintain administrative privileges. Similarly, a removed user could continue to access the system until their session naturally expired.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/pyload/core/api/__init__.py
+++ b/src/pyload/core/api/__init__.py
@@ -1640,7 +1648,10 @@ def remove_user(self, user: str) -> bool:
                 for apikey in keys_to_delete:
                     del self._apikey_cache[apikey]
 
-        return self.pyload.db.remove_user(user)
+        removed = self.pyload.db.remove_user(user)
+        if removed:
+            self._invalidate_user_sessions(user)
+        return removed
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a session invalidation mechanism within the API. Key user management functions (remove_user, change_password, set_user_permission) now call a new `_invalidate_user_sessions` method, which in turn uses a registered invalidator function to clear all active sessions for the specified user. This ensures that any changes to user permissions or account status are immediately reflected in their active sessions.

## GHSA-jq7h-wrvp-3rgx · pyload-ng

2026-10-09 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `SESSION_FIXATION→HIJACK`

### Root cause

The application's REST API endpoints for user privilege revocation, password changes, and user removal did not invalidate existing user sessions. This meant that even after a user's permissions were downgraded, their password was changed, or their account was deleted, their active session remained valid, allowing continued access with the old credentials or privileges.

### Impact

An attacker could maintain access to a user's account even after the legitimate user changed their password or an administrator revoked their privileges. In the case of user deletion, the attacker could continue to use the deleted user's session until it naturally expired, potentially leading to unauthorized access or privilege escalation.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/pyload/core/api/__init__.py
+++ b/src/pyload/core/api/__init__.py
@@ -1648,13 +1659,17 @@ def change_password(self, user: str, oldpw: str, newpw: str) -> bool:
         """
         changes password for specific user.
         """
-        return self.pyload.db.change_password(user, oldpw, newpw)
+        changed = self.pyload.db.change_password(user, oldpw, newpw)
+        if changed:
+            self._invalidate_user_sessions(user)
+        return changed
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a session invalidation mechanism. A `_session_invalidator` callable is added to the API, which is then set to `clear_all_user_sessions` from the web UI helpers. This invalidator is explicitly called after successful password changes, user removals, and user permission updates, ensuring that affected user sessions are immediately terminated.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 5 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, Python, JavaScript
> → GHSA-gj7p-595x-qwf5
> → GHSA-hg7g-56h5-5pqr
> → GHSA-fj52-5g4h-gmq8
> → GHSA-94jp-7776-qj6q

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/pyload/pyload/commit/3e726ba271a6b1015e3a0ea6dc7e46a8f71a62ad)

## GHSA-pfvf-fwfp-25mp · strawberry-graphql

2026-10-09 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from a logical error in how Strawberry GraphQL handled permission checks. When a permission's `has_permission` method returned an awaitable (e.g., a coroutine object from a plain `def` function), the synchronous resolution path treated this awaitable object as a truthy value, effectively bypassing the actual authorization check. This occurred because `iscoroutinefunction` only detects `async def` functions, allowing plain `def` functions returning coroutines to slip through the synchronous path.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authorization checks for GraphQL fields if the permission logic was implemented using a synchronous function that inadvertently returned an awaitable. This could lead to unauthorized access to sensitive data or functionality that should have been protected by the permission.

### Key diff

```
--- a/strawberry/permission.py
+++ b/strawberry/permission.py
@@ -205,8 +208,25 @@ def resolve(
     ) -> Any:
         """Checks if the permission should be accepted and raises an exception if not."""
         for permission in self.permissions:
-            if not permission.has_permission(source, info, **kwargs):
+            has_permission = permission.has_permission(source, info, **kwargs)
+
+            if inspect.isawaitable(has_permission):
+                if inspect.iscoroutine(has_permission):
+                    has_permission.close()
+
+                raise PermissionReturnedAwaitableInSyncContextError(permission)
+
+            if not has_permission:
                 return self._on_unauthorized(permission)
         return next_(source, info, **kwargs)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check using `inspect.isawaitable` to detect if a permission's `has_permission` method returns an awaitable object during synchronous resolution. If an awaitable is detected, it is immediately closed (if it's a coroutine) and a `PermissionReturnedAwaitableInSyncContextError` is raised, preventing the awaitable from being treated as a truthy value and failing closed instead of silently granting access.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 127 occurrences**

## GHSA-vq8p-m3wm-gv5f · pyload-ng

2026-10-09 · Python · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application did not limit the size of incoming requests, allowing an attacker to send arbitrarily large payloads. This could lead to excessive memory consumption or processing time on the server.

### Impact

An attacker could send a large request, causing the server to consume significant resources, potentially leading to a denial of service where legitimate users cannot access the application or the process terminates.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/pyload/webui/app/config.py
+++ b/src/pyload/webui/app/config.py
@@ -9,6 +9,7 @@ class BaseConfig:
     DEBUG = False
     TESTING = False
     SESSION_COOKIE_SAMESITE = "Lax"
+    MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH = 16 * 1024 * 1024  #: 16 Megabytes
     #: Extensions
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH` configuration setting, limiting the maximum size of incoming request bodies to 16 megabytes. This prevents attackers from sending excessively large payloads to exhaust server resources.

## GHSA-j92p-c242-7hfx · pyload-ng

2026-10-09 · Python · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The application allowed unauthenticated access to the `/web/<path:filename>` endpoint, which rendered arbitrary templates. This permitted access to sensitive templates without proper authentication. Additionally, an `AttributeError` typo in the exception handler caused internal exception details to be exposed directly to the user.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could access sensitive web templates, potentially gaining information about the application's structure or internal state. Furthermore, detailed internal error messages, including stack traces or sensitive data from exceptions, could be leaked, aiding further attacks.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/pyload/webui/app/blueprints/app_blueprint.py
+++ b/src/pyload/webui/app/blueprints/app_blueprint.py
@@ -31,6 +31,11 @@ def favicon():
 
 @bp.route("/web/<path:filename>", endpoint="web")
 def render(filename):
+    # Allow login.html without authentication, protect all other files
+    if filename != "login.html" and not is_authenticated():
+        location = flask.url_for("app.login", next="index")
+        return flask.redirect(location)
+
     mimetype = mimetypes.guess_type(filename)[0] or "text/html"
     data = render_template(filename)
     return flask.Response(data, mimetype=mimetype)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an authentication check for the `/web/<path:filename>` endpoint, redirecting unauthenticated users to the login page for any file other than `login.html`. It also modifies the exception handler to provide a generic error message instead of exposing detailed exception attributes, preventing information leakage.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 82 occurrences**

## GHSA-m952-2w3f-6r8h · strawberry-graphql

2026-10-09 · Python · **LOW** 3.7 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the `strawberry-graphql` library's legacy `graphql-ws` protocol handler not properly cleaning up subscription-related resources (tasks and subscription sources) when a subscription completed naturally. This meant that even after a subscription finished, its entry in the `self.subscriptions` and `self.tasks` dictionaries was not removed, leading to resource accumulation.

### Impact

An attacker could initiate a large number of subscriptions that complete naturally. Over time, this would lead to an accumulation of stale entries in the server's internal state, potentially exhausting memory or other resources, and ultimately causing a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/strawberry/subscriptions/protocols/graphql_ws/handlers.py
+++ b/strawberry/subscriptions/protocols/graphql_ws/handlers.py
@@ -215,17 +215,28 @@ async def handle_async_results(
 
         except asyncio.CancelledError:
             await self.send_message(CompleteMessage(type="complete", id=operation_id))
+        finally:
+            self.subscriptions.pop(operation_id, None)
+            self.tasks.pop(operation_id, None)
 
     async def cleanup_operation(self, operation_id: str) -> None:
-        if operation_id in self.subscriptions:
+        result_source = self.subscriptions.pop(operation_id, None)
+        if result_source is not None:
             with suppress(RuntimeError):
-                await self.subscriptions[operation_id].aclose()
-            del self.subscriptions[operation_id]
+                await result_source.aclose()
+
+        task = self.tasks.pop(operation_id, None)
+        if task is None:
+            return
 
-        self.tasks[operation_id].cancel()
+        task.cancel()
         with suppress(BaseException):
-            await self.tasks[operation_id]
-        del self.tasks[operation_id]
+            await task
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `handle_async_results` method to ensure that `self.subscriptions` and `self.tasks` are always cleaned up in a `finally` block, regardless of how the subscription operation concludes. It also refactors `cleanup_operation` to safely remove and close resources, preventing redundant cleanup attempts and ensuring resources are released.

## GHSA-9q47-3cm2-2rp8 · pyload-ng

2026-10-09 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→HEADER`

### Root cause

The application directly used the `X-Forwarded-For` header to determine the client IP address for rate-limiting and logging purposes. This header can be easily manipulated by an attacker, allowing them to spoof their IP address and bypass rate limits or inject arbitrary values into audit logs.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass rate limits, potentially leading to brute-force attacks or denial of service. They could also spoof their IP address in audit logs, hindering incident response and forensic analysis.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/pyload/webui/app/blueprints/app_blueprint.py
+++ b/src/pyload/webui/app/blueprints/app_blueprint.py
-        client_ip = flask.request.headers.get("X-Forwarded-For", "").split(',')[0].strip() or flask.request.remote_addr
+        client_ip = get_client_ip()
--- a/src/pyload/webui/app/__init__.py
+++ b/src/pyload/webui/app/__init__.py
+        if trusted_proxy_count:
+            app.wsgi_app = ProxyFix(app.wsgi_app, x_for=trusted_proxy_count)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `get_client_ip` helper function that correctly determines the client IP by integrating with Werkzeug's `ProxyFix` middleware. This middleware is now configured to trust a specified number of proxies, ensuring that the `X-Forwarded-For` header is only processed from trusted sources.

## GHSA-fr26-jjhm-638c · pyload-ng

2026-10-09 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The application used the `tarfile` module to extract archives without explicitly filtering out dangerous member types like device nodes (e.g., character or block devices) or FIFOs. This allowed an attacker to craft a malicious tar archive containing special files.

### Impact

An attacker could create arbitrary device nodes or FIFOs on the system where the archive is extracted, potentially leading to denial of service, privilege escalation, or other system compromises depending on the context and permissions.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/pyload/plugins/extractors/UnTar.py
+++ b/src/pyload/plugins/extractors/UnTar.py
@@ -70,6 +73,8 @@ def _safe_extractall(self, tar, path=".", members=None, *, numeric_owner=False):
             member_path = os.path.join(path, member.name)
             if not is_within_directory(path, member_path):
                 raise ArchiveError("Attempted Path Traversal in Tar File (CVE-2007-4559)")
+            if member.isdev():
+                raise ArchiveError("Device files are not allowed in TAR archives.")
 
             # Validate symlink targets to prevent symlink escape attacks
             if member.issym() or member.islnk():
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check within the `_safe_extractall` method to explicitly disallow the extraction of device files. If a member is identified as a device file using `member.isdev()`, an `ArchiveError` is raised, preventing its creation.

## GHSA-xv7q-fvmc-jx96 · code.vikunja.io/api

2026-10-09 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The application's OIDC email-fallback account linking mechanism did not properly verify the 'email_verified' claim from the OIDC provider. It allowed linking an OIDC account to an existing local account based solely on the email address, even if the email was not verified by the OIDC provider. This meant an attacker could register with an OIDC provider using a victim's email address (without necessarily owning it) and then link to the victim's local account.

### Impact

An attacker could take over a victim's local account by registering with an OIDC provider using the victim's email address and then logging in via OIDC, effectively linking to and gaining control of the victim's existing account.

### Key diff

```
-	if provider.EmailFallback {
+	emailFallbackAllowed := provider.EmailFallback && bool(cl.EmailVerified)
 	fallbackEmail := ""
-	if provider.EmailFallback {
+	if emailFallbackAllowed {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `boolish` type to correctly parse the `email_verified` claim, which can be a boolean or a string. It then modifies the account linking logic to explicitly check that `EmailVerified` is true before allowing an OIDC account to link to a local account via email fallback. This ensures that only verified emails can be used for account linking.
